# Mandate 2026-09-13 (evening) — the affordable plan, and the ledger of obstacles on the way to it

*The user's words, 13 September, evening: "Ik wil een betaalbaar plan. Ik wil tegen de supervisor kunnen zeggen: als we dit doen op de nieuwe PC en een kleine Snellius aanvraag, dan hebben we in 2027 een neuraal netwerk getraind dat in een pipeline past waar een grote PAK ingaat en een spectrale vorm uitkomt. Blijf, ook als ik slaap, oplossingen zoeken voor de obstakels die we tegenkomen op weg naar dat doel." This note is the working document for that mandate: the goal in one sentence, the budget it must fit, the arithmetic of what the budget buys, and a ledger of obstacles with the best solution found so far, its status, and the test that decides it. It is appended, dated, every time an obstacle moves. It changes no rule of plan 05 by itself; changes it motivates are made as dated revisions and named here.*

## 1. The goal sentence (to be said to the supervisor)

> On the new desktop plus a small Snellius request, by 2027 a neural network is trained that sits in a pipeline where a large PAH goes in and a spectral shape comes out — positions, intensities and drawn widths of its infrared bands — better than the existing predictions on the reach rungs R4–R6, or with a measured error budget that is smaller than theirs (the user's success criterion, P26 memo addendum of 13 September).

Budget: the €5,000 workstation of the hardware note (16 cores, 128 GB, P13, ordered only after evidence and the supervisor's approval) and a small Snellius allocation sponsored by the supervisor. Time: the capstone calendar (defense May 2027, §12).

## 2. What the budget buys, in labels per year (duration table, P26 block; **F = 3.34 measured 14 September**, desktop 2.3–3.8× the laptop, plus a small Snellius share)

| label class | full deck | diagonal-only deck | gradients (if g ≤ 5) |
|---|---|---|---|
| benzene-class (M ≈ 30) | 82–161 | 294–573 | 880–2,866 |
| naphthalene-class (M ≈ 48) | 1.2–2.4 | 6.0–11.8 | 6.5–21.0 |
| pyrene-class (M ≈ 72) | 0.1–0.2 | 0.4–1.6 | 0.4–2.7 |
| coronene-class (M ≈ 102) | 0.0–0.1 | 0.0–0.2 | 0.1–1.3 |

Reading (with the measured F): the truth the network can be trained on in one year is **one or two full naphthalene-class corrections, six to twelve diagonal-only naphthalene-class corrections, a hundred benzene-class corrections, at most one or two thin pyrene-class checks, and a coronene-class check only if g is small** — more if P25 is licensed. Everything above pyrene size that the network says is *reach*, not truth, and is judged on hold-outs and a grounded error budget. This is the honest content of the goal sentence; the arithmetic is remade when g (M2a, this week) is measured.

## 3. The pipeline the sentence describes (what exists, what is missing)

large PAH → DFT Hessian and dipole derivatives (B3LYP/6-31G*, affordable to hundreds of atoms on the desktop; PAHdb did C₃₈₄H₄₈ at 4-31G) → **network** predicts the coupled-cluster correction per mode (positions) and, if lead C delivers, the intensity correction → anharmonic shifts at DFT level as PAHdb Anharmonic does (declared, not corrected) → drawn widths under a declared broadening model → spectral shape with a per-family error budget.

Exists: DFT Hessians and modes (dry-run machinery, plan 02's Hessians, the Module 05 corpus factory), the CC label engine (frozen LNO spaces, checkpointed, resumable), the symmetry prior and probing algebra, Module 05's Transformer scaffold, the scoreboard and u_band machinery. Missing: the labels (F, g, thin decks), the intensity side (lead C), the transfer evidence (T-1/T-2), the network's training recipe on few labels (pre-train on a cheap proxy of the correction, fine-tune on CC labels — the Käser/Bowman form, §4 item 3), and the width model (declared).

## 4. Obstacle ledger (obstacle → best solution so far → status → the test that decides)

1. **Labels cost too much above naphthalene.** Thin decks (diagonal-only 2M energies; P25's free DFT ordering of the couplings; symmetry-pattern products with gradients: 18 gradients recover naphthalene's whole correction if g is small, X14) instead of full decks; tight vs xtight chosen by measurement. *Status:* F tonight; g this week (M2a, pre-registered); P25's licence after the naphthalene DFT dry run (queue item 5). *Decides:* the table in §2 recomputed with measured F and g; plan 06's decision rule (15 October).
2. **Does a correction measured on small molecules say anything about large ones?** Per-family, mode-resolved transfer tests (T-1 benzene→naphthalene, T-2 with a real CC hold-out); X17's finding that in internal coordinates the correction terminates one bond out gives a transferable *type* vocabulary (bond–adjacent bond, bond–angle sharing an atom, one apart). *Status:* T-1 waits for naphthalene's stand-in Hessian (queue item 5); **X18 (type transfer, in-plane block) is written and self-tested on benzene (21:5x), runs the moment item 5 delivers**; sub-obstacle 2a: the out-of-plane block needs a symmetry-covariant sign convention for wags and torsions before its types can transfer (idea: orient every wag to +z and define the torsion's sign by the twist of its two outer atoms, then test on benzene→benzene). *Decides:* RMS ≤ 2.5 cm⁻¹ per judged family (agreed threshold); losing condition in P26 §6.
3. **What the network must learn may be too large for few labels.** Shrink it first with a better functional (lead A: a double hybrid as the DFT half if it removes ≥ 2/3 of the correction; reading thresholds confirmed), then pre-train the network on a cheap proxy of the correction computable for thousands of PAHs on the desktop (the BHHLYP−B3LYP stand-in the Module 05 corpus already plans, or a double-hybrid−B3LYP proxy if lead A shows it is closer to CC) and fine-tune on the CC labels of §2 — the transfer-learning form of Käser et al. 2021 and the Δ-ML form of Qu et al. 2021, applied to Hessian corrections instead of surfaces — this pre-train/fine-tune recipe is already written into Module 05's RECIPE (dated P26 amendment of 13 September: the ωB97X − B3LYP corpus on Hessian QM9 subsets for pre-training, fine-tuning on pipeline B's thin-deck labels); what is new here is only the choice of proxy, decided by lead A's test. *Status:* `probes/dh_diagonal_baseline.py` ready (queue item 5a); the proxy-validity test is the same run (CC vs DH−B3LYP vs BHHLYP−B3LYP per mode at R0 and R1). *Decides:* r ≤ 1/3 (collapse into "double hybrid as DFT half") / ≥ 2/3 (fit or learn the whole correction).
4. **No laboratory truth at large sizes; "better than what exists" must still be grounded.** Hold-out molecules with measured corrections (the thin decks of §2 are the hold-outs), the pipeline's own error budget per family, and one cold resolved measurement where it can be had (lead G, in the letter, §13 item 3). *Status:* criterion agreed; G asked. *Decides:* the supervisor's answer on G; T-2's numbers.
5. **Shape is more than positions: intensities and widths.** Intensities: lead C (dipoles at every probed geometry are free at SCF/MP2 level; HF→MP2 intensity change along each mode tells whether correlation moves intensities where DFT fails, the 6.2/7.7 µm ratio) and DFT intensities as the base; widths: a declared broadening model (absorption at 0 K plus a stated width; astronomical emission profiles are the domain of cascade models and are not promised). *Status (14 Sep):* C is one flag in the probe scripts (the scripts are no longer live; to add before the next deck); the width model is declared in the pilot-note skeleton §C (14 Sep: a fixed 10 cm⁻¹ Lorentzian for figures only; no score depends on it); **measured 14 Sep on line A: the shape score's largest terms are family-weight errors (C–H out-of-plane over-weighted by 24–42 pp) and the missing combination-band intensity (12–24 % of the laboratory intensity in 1650–2000 cm⁻¹), not positions** — intensities and anharmonic combination bands are the first obstacle on the shape objective. *Decides:* whether the HF→MP2 intensity table on benzene/naphthalene shows a correction large enough to learn.
6. **Compute that exists only on paper.** The desktop is a priced configuration, not a purchase (gated on evidence and approval); Snellius is a request the supervisor must sponsor; the laptop is at its memory ceiling at naphthalene. *Status:* F tonight is the evidence for the request's size; the request's content under P26 is naphthalene full plus thin decks, not full decks at pyrene. *Decides:* the user's purchase decision; the supervisor's sponsorship.
7. **Gradients are the biggest lever and no one has printed their price.** M2a measures g in PySCFAD with the shipped engine; M2 (frozen-space gradients, 2–3 weeks) only if g ≤ 20. *Status:* installed, pre-registered, runs after the current run. *Decides:* g ≤ 6 / ≤ 20 / > 20. **Status 17 September, 23:3x:** g **printed** — 6.04 at eight threads for LNO-CCSD(T) at benzene 6-31G on the full energy (cc-pVDZ does not fit the laptop), inside the ≤ 20 gate and near the ≤ 6 band; but M2b (same night) shows PySCFAD's engine does not compute the plan's frozen-space response (5.5 / 8.2 / 26.4 µE_h against a bar of 6), so **M2 — in-house gradients of the frozen-space energies, 2–3 weeks — is the remedy**, and its own g is unmeasured. The route's counting (2k + 1 = 19 gradients at naphthalene, X21) and noise behaviour (X20) stand.
8. **The calendar.** Proposal in September; R1 full deck on Snellius November–December (17–28 Snellius-days at 4 nodes, or the desktop in months); thin decks and benzene-class labels January–March; network training March–April; defense May 2027. *Status:* §12 is still the pre-P26 calendar and is revised after F with the table the user asked for. *Decides:* F and the supervisor's reading time.
9. **Cations.** Astronomically as important as neutrals; the proxy corpus can carry them at DFT level, CC labels need unrestricted local CC (pyscf-forge has `ULNOCCSD_T`, per the API notes of 12 September). *The user, 14 Sep evening ("Onthoud dat we dat gat moeten dichten"):* the cations appear in no row of the affordability collection (benzene and naphthalene measured, pyrene–coronene estimated, the corpus's six timing molecules) — **a standing gap to close, not a price question**: benzene⁺ and naphthalene⁺ get their own rows in the affordability table and the P26 label plan once one cation energy is timed (smoke test on a small radical first, then benzene⁺ in cc-pVDZ on a quiet machine). *Status (14 Sep, 10:1x — checked in the installed software, no computation):* `ULNOCCSD_T` is importable from the installed pyscf-forge LNO module (PySCF 2.14.0, `pyscf/lno/ulnoccsd.py`, 540 lines) and has a shipped unit test (`lno/test/test_ulnoccsd.py`); its (T) part is `ulnoccsd_t_slow.py`, a 294-line NumPy/`lib.einsum` reference kernel adapted from PySCF's `gccsd_t_slow` (uses cotengra if installed), i.e. **without the compiled kernel of the restricted path** — cation labels are possible in software terms today, at an unmeasured price. *Measured 15 Sep 15:26:* **c = 31.0** at benzene, cc-pVDZ tight (164 s neutral, 5,096 s cation, 4.2 GB; ⟨S²⟩ 0.870) — the installed unrestricted path prices every cation deck off the laptop (P27 §5); next: the UCCSD/(T) split (`--no-t`), then the port decision. *Decides:* after M2a, on a quiet WSL: the shipped test (minutes), then one benzene-cation cc-pVDZ tight single point against the neutral's measured time — the cation/neutral cost ratio decides whether cations enter the label budget of §2 or wait for a compiled unrestricted (T).
10. **Anharmonicity stays at DFT level.** Positions = corrected harmonic + DFT anharmonic shift (as PAHdb Anharmonic); declared in §6, the Δ₂ = 0 null row separates the two. *Status (revised 14 Sep, 11:2x):* declared — but the amended shape score shows the anharmonic step is **load-bearing for the shape objective**: PAHdb Anharmonic (column B) scores 19.1 cm⁻¹ against the scaled harmonic column's 45.1 on tetracene, almost entirely through combination-band intensity and resonance-shifted positions. The pipeline therefore needs its own anharmonic step with intensities (pyVPT2 positions, permitted; I6 intensities, to write) before Δ₂ can be seen on shape. *Decides:* the Δ₂ = 0 null column on tetracene against B's 19.1.

## 5. Standing search instruction (the user, 13 September; broadened the same evening)

Whenever the machine is busy or the user is away, the next item is the cheapest untested obstacle in this ledger: desk tests on existing tensors first (X-series), literature checks second, scripts prepared for the queue third; never heavy compute beside the anchor job, never a plan 07 folder without the user, every finding appended here with its date and the sentence that decided it. **And — the user, same evening: "je mag ook iets *vinden*; verplaats jezelf in de schoenen van de wetenschapper" — part of every check is spent asking what could break the goal sentence and what could make it cheaper or stronger: new obstacles, new levers, new falsification tests, new literature, added below as numbered obstacles or as ideas (I-numbers), each with the test that would decide it, ideas labelled as ideas and measurements as measurements.** **Also (the user, 21:3x): search for or make new software** — existing open-source packages that remove an obstacle (licence, capability, repository or DOI verified) and, where none fits, software written here (the user's standing note that we may write our own when existing packages fail), self-tested on existing data; installations and heavy runs only with permission; every software change gets a same-day row in `Software_Changes_Ledger.md`. A two-hourly session task (13 September, 19:4x; job replaced 21:3x) runs this instruction.

## 5a. Obstacles and ideas added by the standing search (dated; ideas are ideas until a test says otherwise)

11. **The labels themselves carry a basis-set error larger than the method error.** (13 Sep, evening.) The anchor is LNO-CCSD(T)/cc-pVTZ; M1 §2.2d measured the canonical curvature's basis dependence at +134/−67/−145 cm⁻¹ per benzene mode from DZ to TZ, two thirds SCF, and decision 33 carries a 3–11 cm⁻¹ basis term against a local-correlation bias of 0.1–0.2 cm⁻¹. A network trained on TZ labels learns TZ truth. *Best solution:* the composite anchor P18 (SCF and MP2 at QZ/5Z are cheap — the 27-point line took nineteen minutes — plus the CC correction at TZ); Semidalas & Martin 2023 (composite methods for harmonic frequencies, abstract) is the literature to read. *Status (corrected 14 Sep, 11:0x — the ledger was behind the README):* P18 was **adopted on 12 September as decision 33**: the anchor energy per point is LNO-CCSD(T)(xtight, TZ) + [MP2(full) − MP2(LNO)] + [MP2/QZ − MP2/TZ] + [SCF/5Z − SCF/TZ], every term a computed difference, under 1 % of an LNO point; the cheap basis line of the same day (`probes/results_m1/BASIS_LINE_scf_mp2.md`) measured the TZ → QZ/5Z terms on benzene's three probed modes: SCF +1.8/+3.8, −2.4/−2.8, −4.6/−3.1 cm⁻¹ (QZ/5Z), MP2 correlation −1.0, −2.4, −8.0 cm⁻¹ (QZ) — 1–8 cm⁻¹ against a frozen-space bias of 0.1–0.2, which is why the composite is the anchor. What remains open is decision 26's term: the basis change of the CCSD(T) − MP2 remainder beyond TZ, unmeasured (a QZ CC point at benzene would measure it; not affordable at naphthalene). The 14 September budget documents already price the composite's terms (60 s MP2/QZ + 353 s SCF/5Z per naphthalene point). *Decides (remaining):* one benzene CC/QZ point per probed mode, if ever affordable; otherwise the term stays a declared bound.
12. **The DFT side of the pipeline at R6 has never been timed by this project.** (13 Sep, evening.) A B3LYP/6-31G* Hessian of C₃₈₄H₄₈ (432 atoms) is the network's *input*; PAHdb computed such Hessians at 4-31G, this project has timed nothing above pyrene. *Best solution:* time a coronene B3LYP Hessian on the laptop after the run (plan 02 has Hessians to pyrene), extrapolate with the measured scaling, and if the desktop cannot do R6-size Hessians in days, the pipeline's input at R6 is PAHdb's own Hessian data or a fragment scheme. *Status (14 Sep, 08:1x — a measurement the project already had):* plan 02 timed on this laptop, psi4 1.11, B3LYP/6-31G* frequency jobs including optimisation: benzene 4.2 min, pyrene 54 min, coronene **174 min** (Compute Budget 2026-09-14 §3.1). Coronene (36 atoms, 24 C) to C₃₈₄H₄₈ (432 atoms) is a factor 12 in atoms; analytic Hessians scale between N³ and N⁴ in this regime, so one R6-size Hessian is 174 min × 12^3–12^4 ≈ 200–3,000 days on the laptop, 60–1,300 days on the desktop by the core-count estimate. **The DFT side at R6 is therefore not a desktop computation either**; the pipeline's input for R6-class molecules is PAHdb's own Hessian-level data where it exists (line A computed C₃₈₄H₄₈ at 4-31G), a fragment scheme, or a neural-network force field (obstacle 14's lever, licence permitting). Layer D of idea I5 is bounded by the same arithmetic: coronene-size pairs are hours, circumcoronene-size (54 C) ≈ 174 min × (66/36)^3.5 ≈ 24 h each on the laptop — a few hundred of them is desktop-months, which fixes layer D's reach at roughly 50–60 carbons. *Decides:* one re-timed coronene Hessian with the plan-05 factory (queue item 3, minutes to hours) to replace the plan-02 number; the exponent from benzene–pyrene–coronene on the same machine.
13. **The pre-training corpus and the size axis above four rings.** (13 Sep, evening; corrected the same evening after the user pointed out that the corpus is *made*, not taken: Module 05's corpus design of 12 September — `modules/05_support_predictor/corpus/DESIGN_2026-09-12.md` — already builds layer A (45 aromatics), layer A′ (868 mono-substituted three- and four-ring cores: anthracene, phenanthrene, pyrene, fluoranthene …, the user's decision) and layer B (4,353 substituted aromatic and heteroaromatic cores up to 26 atoms) with B3LYP and the high functional, and uses Hessian QM9's conjugated class only as layer C. So the first version of this obstacle was wrong: PAHs up to four rings are in the corpus by design.) *What remains open:* the size axis **above four rings** — the reach rungs are coronene-class and larger, and the corpus's largest cores are four rings; whether pre-training that stops at four rings transfers to R4–R6 is exactly the transfer question (obstacle 2), and extending the corpus upward costs B3LYP Hessians whose price at coronene size and above is untimed (obstacle 12). *Best solution:* PAHdb v4.00's species geometries as the source for a layer D of bare PAHs from five rings up, added only if obstacle 12's timing allows. *Status:* layers A/A′/B designed and manifested (11,321 rows), not yet run (the five-molecule timing test is machine-queue item 3). **Measured 14 Sep 07:0x from `corpus/manifest.csv`:** carbon counts per layer — A 6–16 (median 11), A′ 8–17 (median 13), B 3–14 (median 9); layer C carries QM9 labels without SMILES in the manifest (≤ 9 heavy atoms by construction). **The whole corpus tops out at 17 carbon atoms**: nothing at coronene size (24 C), nothing near the reach rungs; the size axis above four rings is not in anyone's Hessian data — ours stops at 17 C, line D's spectra thin out above ≈ 100 C. *Decides:* obstacle 12's hours per Hessian versus size; T-2 for whether four-ring pre-training carries.

14. **A competing "large PAH in → harmonic IR spectrum out" machine-learning pipeline already exists as software (found 13 Sep, 22:1x, authenticated GitHub search).** `zwAstroChem/ML-DH-PAH-IR` (repository created 2026-07-23; README read via the GitHub API): "High-Throughput Computation of Infrared Spectra of Polycyclic Aromatic Hydrocarbons Using a Machine-Learning-Based Double-Harmonic Model" — a neural-network force field (Montes-Campos, Carrete, Bichelmaier, Varela & Madsen, *J. Chem. Inf. Model.* 62, 88–101 (2021), DOI 10.1021/acs.jcim.1c01380) for harmonic frequencies and normal modes plus an electron-passing neural network (Metcalf, Jiang, Spronk, Cheney & Sherrill, *J. Chem. Inf. Model.* 61, 115–122 (2020), DOI 10.1021/acs.jcim.0c01071) for dipole derivatives, charge-state-specific models for neutral, cationic and anionic PAHs, shipped pre-trained. Same organisation as `zwAstroChem/MLMDIR` (Apache-2.0), the code of line C (Mai, Wang, Pan, Schörghuber, Kovács, Carrete & Madsen, *MNRAS* 541, 3073 (2025), item 5). **The accompanying paper was not found tonight in Crossref, OpenAlex or arXiv (arXiv's API rate-limited the last query); this is a software record only, and the repository states no licence.** *Why it matters:* it is the goal sentence's shape, at DFT-training-data quality, already built — the network the user wants must be *better than this*, not only than PAHdb; and its training data are presumably DFT (to be read in the paper), which is exactly where this project's coupled-cluster labels differ. *Lever:* an NNFF gives Hessians of R6-size PAHs in seconds — if licensed and validated against B3LYP on the ladder molecules, it removes obstacle 12 (the DFT-side cost at R6) for the pipeline's *input*; without a licence it is an opponent line D for Module 02. *Status, 22:3x (the user searched arXiv by hand — nothing under "double-harmonic"; the citing works of Mai 2025 in OpenAlex then gave the cluster):* the ML-DH repository's own paper is still not found, but **the same group (Zhao Wang and co-workers) published three DFT-trained PAH-IR predictors in 2026, all Crossref-verified, all open access:** He, J.; Mai, X.; Wang, Z., "Charge-aware machine learning for the infrared spectra of interstellar polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons", *A&A* 708, A335 (2026), DOI 10.1051/0004-6361/202659248 — a neural network on topological fingerprints with learnable charge embeddings, **12,599 species, four charge states (anions to di-cations), up to 150 carbon atoms, "near-DFT accuracy", accuracy for larger molecules "limited by the available training data"** (abstract); Tang, G.; He, J.; Wang, Z.; Qiu, D., "Graph neural network prediction of infrared spectra of interstellar polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons", *MNRAS* 546, stag283 (2026), DOI 10.1093/mnras/stag283 — GNNs 10,000× faster than quantum chemistry, best for 20–40 carbons, accuracy decreasing for larger molecules (abstract); Liu, Y.; Wang, Z.; Qiu, D., "AstroSpec-LLM: a large language model framework for high-throughput infrared spectral prediction of interstellar PAHs", *MNRAS* 549, stag893 (2026), DOI 10.1093/mnras/stag893 — transformer on molecular strings fine-tuned on 24,146 PAH spectra (abstract); plus Xu, X.; Mai, X.; Wang, Z., *Phys. Scr.* 101, 306004 (2026), DOI 10.1088/1402-4896/ae8c84 (ML dipole moments of non-equilibrium PAHs; record only). **What this settles for the goal sentence:** "better than what exists" now has named, dated opponents that already deliver charge-aware spectra of PAHs to 150 carbons at DFT accuracy and at ML speed. They reproduce DFT, errors included; none carries a coupled-cluster label or a per-band error budget — that is the whole difference, and it must be *measured* against them (Module 02 line D; the shape hold-outs of I3). Their weak point by their own abstracts is the largest molecules ("limited by the available training data"), which is exactly where a physics-shaped correction (X17/I4: transferable local types) rather than a fingerprint would have to earn its place. **Lever:** their training sets (12,599 and 24,146 DFT spectra) are the proxy-corpus scale of obstacle 3/13; if the data are public (A&A and MNRAS are open access; data-availability statements to be read), they are pre-training material at zero compute — obstacle 13's size axis above four rings may be covered by someone else's DFT. *Decides:* reading the three papers in full (open access — allowed to fetch; the user's download permission is asked for the PDFs); Module 02's line-D entry with version-frozen predictions; whether their datasets are downloadable and under which licence. Related prior art for the shape step, Crossref-verified: Pracht, Pillai, Kapil, Csányi, Gönnheimer, Vondrák, Margraf & Wales, *J. Chem. Theory Comput.* 20, 10986–11004 (2024), DOI 10.1021/acs.jctc.4c01157 — composite double-harmonic IR spectra from semiempirical and transferable ML potentials (abstract).
15. **A learned exchange-correlation functional at meta-GGA cost claiming beyond-double-hybrid accuracy exists as open software (lead A, found the same search).** `ESEberhard/ML4DFT` (MIT; egxc, di-loss, SAIL; JAX): Gao, Eberhard & Günnemann, "Learning Equivariant Non-Local Electron Density Functionals", arXiv:2410.07972 (ICLR 2025 per the README); Eberhard, Thiede, Aldossary, Burger, Gao, Bhethanabotla, Aspuru-Guzik & Günnemann, "Derivative Informed Learning of Exchange-Correlation Functionals", arXiv:2606.04279 (ICML 2026 per the README) — arXiv records verified through OpenAlex. *Why it matters:* lead A's "learn the functional, not the matrix" has a maintained open implementation; whether such functionals are trained or tested on vibrational Hessians is unread (abstracts not yet fetched). *Status (14 Sep 07:0x, abstracts read via OpenAlex):* both papers train on **energies** — DI-Loss distils B3LYP/def2-SVP targets into O(N³) learned functionals, supervising first and second derivatives of the energy *with respect to the density matrix* (not nuclear Hessians); EG-XC reconstructs CCSD(T) energies on the MD17 set by differentiating through the SCF solver. **No vibrational frequency or nuclear-Hessian test in either abstract**; a test of a learned functional's Hessian against the probed CC correction would be this project's to run. Recorded as lead-A software with that gap; no installation. *Decides:* whether a learned functional's Hessian on benzene lands closer to the probed CC correction than B2PLYP (the same r-reading as lead A's test).

16. **The corpus's DFT grid may be too coarse for the labels it produces (14 Sep, 10:1x — a measurement the timing test made on its own).** *Status 16:3x — largely resolved, measured:* on the four test molecules without degenerate or near-zero modes the dense grid moves no mode by more than 0.40–0.56 cm⁻¹ (naphthalene per mode: max 0.41, RMS 0.11 over 48 modes); the two large values, benzene 21.4 and diphenylacetylene 28.3, belong to molecules with degenerate pairs and torsions; production grid (75, 302) kept; the benzene per-mode rerun (10 min, new worker) names the mode after M2a (`notes/Timing_Note_2026-09-14_Corpus_Factory.md` §3). The factory's `--grid-check` recomputes each molecule's B3LYP/6-31G* Hessian on a (99, 590) grid beside the corpus grid (75, 302). Benzene: max |ΔH| 0.0102 E_h/bohr², **max |Δν| 21.4 cm⁻¹** over the 30 projected modes (`corpus/molecules/A_8448043181/result.json`; only the maximum is stored, so the mode is not yet known — a degenerate pair split by the coarse grid is the likeliest reading, a real 20 cm⁻¹ grid error the worst). Either way it is eight times the 2.5 cm⁻¹ margin and would enter every proxy label (ωB97X − B3LYP) unless it cancels between the two functionals on the same grid. The dense grid costs 2.0× (398 vs 195 s at benzene). *Test (after the timing test, quiet machine; one Hessian per grid, minutes):* per-mode Δν between the two grids for B3LYP and for ωB97X at benzene and azulene, and the Δν of the *difference* ωB97X − B3LYP; if the difference cancels below 1 cm⁻¹ the corpus keeps (75, 302); if not, the corpus grid becomes (99, 590) and the corpus price doubles (a dated amendment to `DESIGN_2026-09-12.md` and the duration note). *Decides:* those numbers; nothing in the running test is changed. **Azulene's grid check (done 10:11): max |Δν| 0.56 cm⁻¹, max |ΔH| 0.0003 E_h/bohr²** — forty times smaller than benzene's; so the corpus grid is adequate for a low-symmetry molecule and benzene's 21.4 cm⁻¹ is most likely the coarse Lebedev grid breaking the degeneracy of an E-pair in D₆h (the (75, 302) grid is not rotationally invariant), which affects the high-symmetry minority of the corpus. The per-mode test at benzene stays queued; the corpus grid stays (75, 302) unless it fails.
17. **The anharmonic step that the shape score demands has no open implementation with intensities in this pipeline's ecosystem (14 Sep, 10:1x, software search — the two-hourly task's part C).** The shape score's real deficit (09:5x) is combination-band intensity above 1650 cm⁻¹ (matrix 6–13 %, line A 0 %). Found, read through the GitHub API, nothing installed: **pyVPT2** (`philipmnel/pyvpt2`, BSD-3-Clause, conda-forge, pushed 2026-05-16; Nelson & Sherrill, *J. Chem. Phys.* **162**, 032501 (2025), DOI 10.1063/5.0251445, Crossref-verified, item 83) computes VPT2 fundamentals, overtone and combination-band **positions** from psi4 or QCEngine Hessians with Fermi-resonance identification and a resonance solver, distributable through QCFractal — but its intensities are the **harmonic** ones passed through (`harmonic_intensity` in its schema; no dipole second derivatives). **ViBra** (`raphafe96/ViBra`, Fortran VSCF/VCI, pushed 2026-09-11, arXiv:2607.22850 per its README — not found in OpenAlex today, unverified; no licence in the API record) does compute infrared intensities from first- and second-order dipole derivatives, but only from **ORCA 6.1's** VPT2 output, and ORCA is neither open source nor in the pipeline. `ntBre/spectro` (a SPECTRO reimplementation, positions), `SebRoLENS/orca-ped-analyzer` and `physicien/parser_vpt2` (ORCA output parsers) complete the picture. **So the psi4/PySCF world has open VPT2 positions (pyVPT2) and no open VPT2 intensities**; PAHdb Anharmonic's combination bands came from SPECTRO with Gaussian force fields. *Consequence:* the Δ₂ = 0 null column of the shape score (positions) can be produced with pyVPT2 once its installation is permitted (conda-forge, into the qc environment, psi4 1.11 ≥ 1.8; a Software Changes Ledger row on the day); the **intensity** side is idea I6. *Size ceiling, arithmetic only:* pyVPT2's semi-diagonal quartic field needs of the order of 2 × (3N − 6) displaced Hessians — coronene ≈ 200 Hessians × 3 h ≈ 25 laptop-days, a desktop-week at 6-31G*; feasible for the five shape hold-outs, not above them (obstacle 12 again). *Decides:* permission to install pyVPT2; the benzene run against PAHdb Anharmonic v1.00's benzene positions (same-method check at the level of the resonance treatment).
- **I6 (software idea, 14 Sep 10:1x — "make new software" under the standing instruction; nothing written yet): VPT2 infrared intensities on top of pyVPT2's force field.** psi4 returns the dipole gradient with every analytic Hessian, and pyVPT2 already visits the displaced geometries along each normal mode to build the cubic and semi-diagonal quartic field by finite differences of Hessians; differentiating the dipole gradients along the same displacements gives the second dipole derivatives (and, from the paired displacements, the third) at **zero extra quantum chemistry**. With the cubic force constants and those dipole derivatives, the GVPT2 intensities of fundamentals, overtones and two-quantum combination bands follow the closed formulae of Bloino & Barone, *J. Chem. Phys.* **136**, 124108 (2012), DOI 10.1063/1.3695210 (item 84), reviewed with the resonance treatment in Bloino, Biczysko & Barone, *J. Phys. Chem. A* **119**, 11862 (2015), DOI 10.1021/acs.jpca.5b10067 (item 85) — both Crossref-verified, abstracts not yet read. A module of a few hundred lines that reads pyVPT2's displacement results and returns anharmonic sticks with intensities. *Test that decides it:* on benzene and naphthalene, the combination-band intensity share in 1650–2000 cm⁻¹ against the argon-matrix libraries (Module 02 experimental) and against PAHdb Anharmonic v1.00 where it lists intensities; the win condition is the matrix share within a factor 1.5 where line A gives zero. *Why it matters for the goal sentence:* it is the cheapest route to the one shape deficit that both intensity measurements agree on, it is pipeline-native (psi4, BSD-3 upstream), and it turns the Δ₂ = 0 null column into a full spectral shape. Cost before writing: reading the two papers' formulae (a desk day); writing and self-testing on benzene: two or three days; runs: the pyVPT2 displacements themselves (benzene minutes, naphthalene an hour, coronene a desktop-week). Waits for the user's permission to install pyVPT2 and for the decision whether this is plan-05 work or a plan-07 module.
- **I7 (lever, 14 Sep 11:4x — measured inputs, probe pre-registered; the largest lever found so far): run the coupled-cluster deck in cc-pVDZ and carry the basis step with MP2 plus a transferred beyond-MP2 increment.** Measured: LNO-CCSD(T) tight benzene 180 s (DZ) against 2,087 s (TZ), factor 11.6; naphthalene TZ tight 41,375 s → DZ ≈ 1 h (estimate) → a full naphthalene deck ≈ 20 laptop-days / ≈ 7 desktop-days instead of 226 / 759, i.e. **twenty to forty naphthalene-class labels per desktop-year instead of one or two**, and thin pyrene-class decks in days. The price: benzene's beyond-MP2 DZ → TZ increment is +7.9 / +1.0 / −6.0 cm⁻¹ on the three probed families (M1 §2.2d; nearly all CCSD, the (T) share −2.6 / −0.4 / +2.7), so it must transfer per family or type. *Test:* **probe M3**, `notes/PreRegistration_2026-09-14_M3_DZ_Anchored_Decks.md` — naphthalene, one mode per judged family, 5 points in both bases, tight, ≈ 13 laptop-hours (DZ) + ≈ 6 laptop-days (TZ); win ≤ 2.5 cm⁻¹ per family, lose > 5 or a sign flip. Literature (item 86): Chan & Ho 2023 reach 2 cm⁻¹ of CBS harmonic frequencies at DZ cost with CCSD(T)-F12 + G3(MP2)-type terms — not available in the open stack and incompatible with the frozen LNO spaces; the pipeline's version is the transferred/learned increment. *Order:* after M2a; the user chooses M3 or the σ-run first.
- **I8 (checked 14 Sep 11:3x, little yield): published coupled-cluster harmonic force fields as free labels.** Crossref: the CCSD(T) harmonic-frequency benchmark sets are small molecules (Tew, Klopper, Heckert & Gauss 2007, item 88; Howard & Tschumper 2015, item 87; HFREQ2014 in Chan & Ho 2023) — no aromatic beyond, at most, benzene; the aromatic literature found is MP2-level (Simandiras, Handy & Amos 1988, furan/pyrrole/thiophene) or anharmonic-intensity work (Burcl, Carter & Handy 2004). Not pursued; a benzene CC harmonic set, if found, becomes one consistency check of R0, not a label source.
- **I9 (idea, 14 Sep): label-efficient selection.** With labels this scarce, *which* molecules and modes are labelled matters: choose them by the disagreement of an ensemble of corpus-pretrained networks (active learning) rather than by a fixed ladder. *Test at zero coupled-cluster cost:* on the benzene-class proxy labels of the corpus (ωB97X − B3LYP as the stand-in truth), compare random against greedy-by-disagreement selection at equal label count; if greedy halves the hold-out error at 20 labels, the thin-deck molecule list of P26 is chosen this way (a dated amendment).
- **I10 (small software idea, 14 Sep): a second DFT–DFT stand-in for T-1 at zero compute.** The factory's naphthalene job (today) yields B3LYP and ωB97X Hessians; an adapter `factory → stageA_hessians.npz` lets X18 run benzene → naphthalene on ωB97X − B3LYP beside the BHHLYP − B3LYP stand-in of queue item 5, a robustness check of the type-transfer claim against the choice of stand-in. About sixty lines; written when item 5 has run.
- **I11 (software idea, 14 Sep): the anharmonic intensity shares from a semi-empirical potential.** pyVPT2 accepts any QCEngine program; GFN2-xTB Hessians cost seconds, so a tetracene-size quartic field is minutes. If its combination-band *share* in 1650–2000 cm⁻¹ lands within a factor 1.5 of the matrix (10.7 % for tetracene), the shape score's largest deficit is fixable at negligible cost for every hold-out and the DFT-level anharmonic step is reserved for positions. Needs `xtb` (permission; ledger row). *Test:* tetracene, GFN2-xTB VPT2 shares against matrix and column B.
- **I12 (compute idea, 14 Sep; for the user, no commitment): a rented 16-core / 128 GB machine as the bridge** between now and the desktop's approval — label production and the corpus run start in October instead of after a purchase decision; **price verified 14 Sep 12:3x** (Hetzner's price-adjustment page, docs.hetzner.com, effective 15 June 2026, excl. VAT): the 16-core / 128 GB DDR5 ECC model AX102-1 is **€257.30 per month + €129 setup**, the supply-dependent AX102-1-LTD €157.30 per month — so a bridge of six months costs €1,100–1,700 excl. VAT, of the order of half a desktop, and the earlier guess of €100–150 was too low. The software stack is the WSL one. A pay-per-deck alternative (hourly cloud instances, 8 dedicated vCPUs and 32 GB per anchor job) is unpriced. *Decides:* the user's view on a monthly cost against months of calendar; the pay-per-deck price to verify only if the user wants the option.
- **I13 (strategy, 14 Sep, from the amended shape score): where the shape claim is won.** PAHdb Anharmonic covers only tetracene among the five shape molecules; on coronene, peropyrene, ovalene and HBC the standing column is the scaled harmonic A (amended 30.0 / 14.9 / 28.2 / 29.9 cm⁻¹). A Δ₂ = 0 column with pyVPT2 positions and I6 (or I11) intensities that reproduces B-like physics beats A there by a factor ≈ 2 before any coupled-cluster label — so the shape objective is won by the anharmonic step on four molecules, and the network's coupled-cluster correction is judged on positions at R0–R1 and on the mode-resolved transfer tests, each claim on its own evidence.
- **I1 (idea, 13 Sep evening): MP2 as the middle rung of a three-level transfer chain.** B3LYP → MP2 (DF-MP2 Hessians are affordable to pyrene size on the desktop) → CC (few). M1 §2.2d found MP2 captures 65–109 % of the correlation part of the curvature's basis dependence, so MP2−B3LYP may be a better proxy of CC−B3LYP than BHHLYP−B3LYP. Caution: MP2 with Pople bases gives imaginary out-of-plane frequencies for benzene (Moran et al. 2006) — cc-pVTZ or at least 6-31G* with care. *Test:* `mp2` added to `probes/dh_diagonal_baseline.py`'s functional list (same displaced points, no extra design), read with the same r-thresholds against R0/R1; if MP2−B3LYP is closer to CC−B3LYP than the double hybrid, the proxy corpus is MP2-based.
- **I2 (idea, 13 Sep evening): the deck decomposes its own correction for free.** The engine prints E_corr(MP2), E_corr(CCSD) and E_corr(CCSD(T)) per fragment; every deck therefore yields three corrections — MP2−DFT, CCSD−DFT, CCSD(T)−DFT — at no extra cost. *Test (after R0, respecting the seal):* which component carries the nonlocality of X9 and the Kekulé mode; if the (T) part is smooth and transferable, thin decks could stop at CCSD for many molecules and learn (T) (Ruth, Gerbig & Schreiner 2022, item 37, learned the (T) increment). Nothing touched before the pilot's pre-registration.
- **I3 (lever, 13 Sep evening): cold gas-phase *shapes* exist beyond pyrene.** Lemmens, Rijs & Buma 2021 tabulate jet-cooled positions **and relative intensities** for coronene, peropyrene, ovalene and hexa-peri-benzocoronene (C₄₂H₁₈), 3–100 µm; Lemmens et al. 2019 for naphthalene to pentacene. At ≈ 1 % bandwidth they cannot decide positions at 2.5 cm⁻¹, but the *relative-intensity pattern* is exactly the "spectral shape" of the goal sentence, on molecules of R3 size and above. *Use:* shape hold-outs for the network at zero cost (the coronene column is transcribed; the other three are transcribable from the same table). *Test:* a shape metric pre-registered before any network output exists — **done 14 Sep: `notes/PreRegistration_2026-09-14_Shape_Score_Cold_Spectra.md`** (10 cm⁻¹ bins, earth mover's distance per family — line D's own metric — plus the family-weight error; columns A, B, D, Δ₂ = 0, P, N; losing and winning conditions fixed; the three untranscribed GrandPAH species to transcribe when no anchor job runs).
- **I5 (idea, 14 Sep morning): a layer D of bare PAHs above 17 carbons for the factory.** Source: PAHdb v4.00 species geometries (Module 02 holds the library; ≈ 4,000 species, coronene family and larger, cations included). Content: B3LYP/6-31G* Hessians plus the high-functional side, as layers A/A′; cost unknown until obstacle 12 times a coronene Hessian. *Decides:* hours per Hessian at 24, 32, 42, 54 C on the laptop or desktop; if a coronene pair costs hours, a few hundred species are a desktop-month and the size axis to ≈ 50 C becomes ours; beyond that the pipeline's input at R6 is PAHdb's own Hessian data or a fragment scheme.
- **I4 (design idea, 13 Sep evening): let the network predict internal-coordinate force-constant corrections by local type, not per-mode shifts.** Modes do not correspond across molecules; X17's types (bond–adjacent bond, bond–angle sharing an atom, one apart) do, and the correction terminates one bond out. The mode shifts follow by one transform. *Test:* X17's type transfer benzene→naphthalene (after queue item 5); if it passes at 2.5 cm⁻¹, Module 05's target object changes by a dated amendment.

- **I14 (lever, 14 Sep 12:3x — measured on existing data at zero cost; a deck-design amendment for the user): one energy instead of a ± pair for every pattern confined to a non-totally-symmetric irrep.** The response R_s = ½[ΔE(+p) + ΔE(−p)] − ΔE(0) exists to cancel the force term Δ₁·p (Round 9 item 1) — but at a symmetric equilibrium the gradient is totally symmetric, so for a pattern p inside a non-totally-symmetric one-dimensional irrep Δ₁·p = 0 and E(+p) = E(−p) identically (a group operation maps p to −p), and one energy gives R_s exactly. With the symmetry prior (decision 11) the deck senses Δ₂ block by block anyway, so irrep-pure patterns cost nothing in design. Naphthalene (D₂h, all irreps one-dimensional; 9 a_g + 3 b_1g + 4 b_2g + 8 b_3g + 4 a_u + 8 b_1u + 8 b_2u + 4 b_3u): free elements 189, of which 45 in the a_g block; energies at one per free element ≈ **234 instead of 378** (−38 %) — on top of I7 (DZ), a naphthalene deck ≈ 12 laptop-days / ≈ 4 desktop-days. For D₆h molecules (benzene, coronene, HBC) the one-dimensional irreps halve exactly and the degenerate pairs for the component that a σ_v reflects into its negative (a basis choice), roughly half. *Measured now* (`probes/m1_odd_part_symmetry.py` → `probes/results_m1/ODD_PART_SYMMETRY_mode18.md`, differences only): the odd part E(+q) − E(−q) along benzene mode 18 (b_2u) is, canonical CCSD(T)/TZ, 0.99 (SCF) − 1.10 (correlation) = −0.11 µE_h at q = 1, linear in q — a residual force, i.e. the DFT geometry or mode direction is off D₆h at the 1 µE_h level (removable by symmetrising the geometry before the deck); arm C (fresh spaces) reproduces the canonical odd part to 0.01 µE_h at every threshold, arm A (frozen transported spaces, the deck's arm) breaks the symmetry by 10.9 µE_h in its LNO piece at TZ tight but by only **−0.55 µE_h in the composite energy** (decision 33's quantity) and −0.18 at xtight; DZ tight −1.14; DZ *normal* −103 µE_h (the normal threshold is unusable for this, as its curvature already said). So a single-sided response carries an error of half the odd part, **≤ 0.3 µE_h at tight in the composite**, below σ₄ of arms B/C (1.3–2.3 µE_h) and two orders below the curvature bias (48–85 µE_h) — the halving is admissible at tight and xtight. *Second yield:* the pairs that are kept (a few per irrep) have an odd part that is pure local-correlation noise plus the fixed geometric term, i.e. **a free σ(R_s) witness inside every deck**, part of what decision 35's σ-run buys. *What changes if adopted:* the K-counting rule "a ± pair counts 2" (Round 9 item 1, probes README item 1) becomes "one energy per non-totally-symmetric pattern, a pair per totally symmetric pattern and per noise witness" — a dated amendment of the Ladder and of `dryrun_dft_delta_recovery.py` / `q7_probing_licence.py` (the pattern generator must draw irrep-pure patterns, which the symmetry-prior script's irrep assignment already supplies); the diagonal-cubic bonus probe loses nothing (φ_iii = 0 by symmetry for these modes). *Test before adoption:* the DFT dry run on naphthalene (queue item 5) with irrep-pure single-sided patterns against the direct Δ₂ within τ₇ — the same test that admits the symmetry prior. For the user at 16:30. *(Adopted as decision 37 the same evening. Measured 15 Sep 02:5x on M3's first naphthalene mode: with the factory geometry, D₂h to only 4–7 × 10⁻⁵ bohr, the odd part is 23–34 µE_h per unit q — so geometry symmetrisation to ≤ 10⁻⁸ bohr and irrep projection are hard prerequisites, not refinements; log entry of 02:52.)*

- **22 Sep, 14:5x — geometry term validated (HF, six modes, ≈ 10 %) and applied to the composite: C–H stretches land on CCSD(T) (MAE 52 → 14); two b2g modes and a ≈ −10 in-plane offset remain.** **The geometry term applied to all modes (`r0_geometry_check_oop.py`, `results_m1/r0_geometry_term_cc.json`).** (1) HF validation of the first-order rule Δω_i = ½ Σ_k φ_iik ΔQ_k with the B3LYP cubics of the T3 QFF, against HF/cc-pVTZ curvatures measured at HF's own a1g minimum: mode 4 −72 predicted / −65 measured, 5 −9 / −8, 10 −33 / −27, 6 −48 / −43, 18 +66 / +74, 29 +114 / +111 cm⁻¹ — the rule holds to about 10 % for out-of-plane, Kekulé and C–H stretch alike. (2) The composite's own a1g gradient at the B3LYP geometry, read from the deck's ± points (odd part of E(q) for modes 11 and 29): breathing +118, C–H +1,363 µE_h per unit q → relaxation q₁₁* = −0.026, q₂₉* = −0.095, i.e. r_CH shorter by 0.0046 Å (HF: 0.0124), the expected CC-versus-B3LYP/6-31G* difference. (3) The term added to ω′ against the literature CCSD(T): **C–H stretches 3187 / 3201 / 3211 against 3210 / 3212 / 3218 (MAE 52 → 14)**; a2u ν11 +27 → +2 and e1g ν10 +14 → −4; overall MAE 18.5 → 13.8. Not repaired: the b2g pair ν4 (−36) and ν5 (−45), e2u ν17 (−13 → −28), and the in-plane modes now sit a uniform ≈ −10 below the literature column (7.3 → 10.2 MAE, mean −10). So the geometry term is real, predictable from the cheap cubics and one high-level gradient, and it is the whole story for the C–H stretches; what remains is a ≈ −10 cm⁻¹ offset of the composite against this particular literature column (basis of their ref. 26 unknown to us; our composite is cc-pVTZ with frozen core) and two b2g out-of-plane modes 35–45 low that neither thresholds nor geometry explain — the next suspects are the diagonal-only reading (b2g modes mix with each other) and the composite's frozen-core / basis level, both testable with the existing data plus a few energies.
- **22 Sep, 14:2x — the R0 disagreement explained: not the LNO thresholds, the geometry.** **Xtight (1e-7 / 1e-8) changes nothing:** mode 29 (a1g C–H stretch) ω′ 3173.2 → 3173.2, mode 5 (b2g out-of-plane) 680.5 → 679.5 cm⁻¹ (`R0_XTIGHT_READING_2026-09-22.md`; 9 energies, 2264 s reference, ≈ 5.5 h). LNO truncation is not the cause. **The cause is the geometry.** The deck measures every method's curvature at the B3LYP/6-31G* geometry; the literature harmonics sit at each method's own minimum, and a C–H stretch's curvature changes by tens of cm⁻¹ per hundredth of an ångström. Test with HF/cc-pVTZ, where the effect is largest (`r0_geometry_check.py`, hel1-14, 3 min): the HF a1g minimum lies at q_CH = −0.255, q_breathing = +0.733 relative to the B3LYP geometry (r_CH shorter by 0.0124 Å, energy 1,761 µE_h lower); the curvature along mode 29 rises from 3224.8 at the B3LYP geometry to **3336.2 at HF's own a1g minimum (+111.4 cm⁻¹)**. And the first-order prediction from our own B3LYP cubic constants (T3 QFF: φ₂₉,₂₉,₂₉ = −803.7, φ₂₉,₂₉,₁₂ = +31.0 cm⁻¹) times that displacement is **+113.8** — the mechanism is quantitative. For CCSD(T) the same arithmetic with r_CH shorter by 0.004–0.005 Å (the usual CC-versus-B3LYP/6-31G* difference) gives 3206–3215 against the literature 3218; the in-plane modes hardly move under this relaxation, which is why they agreed. Control: B3LYP/6-31G* with pyscf sits 0.038 in q_CH from the psi4 minimum and its curvature moves by −15.9, again as the cubics predict. **What this means for the pipeline (proposal, not a decision):** a corrected harmonic frequency is the curvature at the low-level geometry *plus a first-order geometry term* — the high-level gradient at the low-level geometry, projected on the low-level modes, times the low-level cubic constants (Δω_i ≈ ½ Σ_k φ_iik ΔQ_k, ΔQ_k = −g_k/ω_k in reduced coordinates). The term needs one high-level gradient per molecule, not a Hessian, and the cubics come from the same two-route QFF that gives the anharmonic shifts. The corpus labels ΔH(at the B3LYP geometry) carry the same term; the ΔH model would predict the curvature part and the gradient part separately, or the label is redefined at the high-level minimum. This goes to the user for the 28 September table and as a possible decision 48; the R0 numbers in the table are presented as curvatures at the B3LYP geometry with the geometry term named.

So the columns are what they claim to be; what is open is why the LNO composite sits 45 cm⁻¹ under the literature CCSD(T) for C–H stretches and scatters ±30 for out-of-plane modes while the in-plane modes land within 2–8. - **21 Sep, 06:2x — the real cause of the benzene VPT2 failure: finite-difference noise in the semi-diagonal quartic constants, not the degeneracies.** Own software `probes/qff_from_hessians.py` (the user: "Schrijf de software maar"): re-assembles φ_ijk and φ_iijj from the 61 cached Hessians in reduced normal coordinates with an independent harmonic analysis, keeps the two finite-difference routes of every φ_iijj, and evaluates VPT2 with the Mills formulas (verified term by term against pyVPT2; the fundamentals agree to 0.1 cm⁻¹ on the same constants). Diagnostic (`results_vpt2/qff_benzene_2026-09-21.md`): the two routes disagree by a median of 22 cm⁻¹, 90th percentile 107, maximum 1,265 cm⁻¹, against constants of at most 590 — the quartic constants are noise. Reason: psi4 has **no analytic Hessian for B3LYP** (1.11 lists analytic Hessians for HF and LDA-type functionals only), so every displaced 'Hessian' is itself a 3-point finite difference of gradients (0.005 bohr), and pyVPT2's default DISP_SIZE 0.05 in reduced coordinates (≈ 0.004 Å) then divides that noise by 0.0025. pyVPT2's own consistency check compares φ[i,j] with φ[j,i] *after* averaging the two routes and therefore always reports 'no inconsistencies'. Two facts for the record: (a) the corpus deck's line 'hessian: analytic, both functionals' is wrong in the same way — psi4's B3LYP/ωB97X Hessians are FD of gradients (harmless at harmonic level, ≈ 0.1 cm⁻¹; to correct in the deck text); (b) the degenerate-pair asymmetry is a symptom of this noise, not of a missing symmetric-top treatment — that question returns once the constants are clean. **Test launched on hel1-14** (kept by the user): two reruns, DISP_SIZE 0.20 with psi4 3-point and with 5-point internal Hessians, 8 threads each, ≈ 9 h wall, ≈ €2; the route-disagreement statistic is the pre-stated meter (expected ≥ 16× smaller at δ = 0.20).**

Rust rejected: it would mean rewriting integrals and coupled cluster with no AD, months to years for a language. The (T) port (decision 41) stays in C, the language of pyscf's own kernels. Two items for M2's pre-registration: enforce float64 (JAX defaults to float32; check PySCFAD's setting), and use checkpointed recomputation for the tape as pyscfad/lno does. **Not started:** M2 begins after the anchor verdict (~22-23 Sep) and on the user's word, with a pre-registered done-criterion (gradient matches finite differences of our own energies to a stated tolerance; its own g printed). (ii) **The q⁴ law is measured, not assumed:** the even part of the response is q² + q⁴ to within the noise (residual 0.00–0.0097 µE_h, 0.0–1.6 σ), so no q⁶ of consequence and halving the amplitude divides the contamination by exactly 16. The finding and blog post 5 carry dated corrections. (ii) **No truth-free score can rank widths**, only detect that they disagree; the honest options are an a-priori resonance criterion, a bought validation set on a small molecule, or dropping the band (w = 3200 costs 4.06 cm⁻¹ against 3.37 at w = 25 — the band buys very little). (iii) **No width rescues the deck:** the ladder spans 0.82–4.06 cm⁻¹ where 0.5 is needed, so **the width question is blocked on the amplitude test**, not free-standing desk work as listed this morning. - **14 Sep, 09:1x — finding (obstacle 5 sharpened, measured):** the shape score's column A (PAHdb v4.00 as served) against the five jet-cooled lists gives intensity-weighted EMD 14.5 (coronene), 16.6 (ovalene), 18.2 (peropyrene), 22.9 (tetracene) cm⁻¹ — and the errors are not position errors: line A puts **24–42 percentage points too much intensity into the C–H out-of-plane family** and **none into the 1650–2000 cm⁻¹ region, where the laboratory lists carry 12–24 % of their intensity in combination bands** (Lemmens 2019's finding, now a number per molecule).
